\displaystyle d\hskip 0.50003pt\Big(\,{i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptD\psi^{m}}\,\Big)\,=\,D\hskip 0.24994pt\Big(\,{i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptD\psi^{m}}\,\Big)\,

\displaystyle=\,i\hskip 1.00006ptD{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\wedge D\psi^{m}+i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptD^{2}\psi^{m}\,.

(69)

Namely,

i\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 1.00006ptF^{i}{}_{j}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}=q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}\,\leftrightarrow\,i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}=-\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{mn}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{mn}
.

Note that we could also have employed the auxiliary Lorentz-valued connection
\bar{D}
,
in which case the covariant symplectic perturbation develops a torsion term as well.

Poisson Brackets

On a related note,
computation shows that
the Poisson brackets between
(x^{\mu},p_{m},\psi^{m})

are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{p_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,&=\,E^{\mu}{}_{r}(x)\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{r}},{p_{s}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,&=\,-p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega^{k}{}_{rs}(x)-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{mn}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{mn}{}_{rs}(x)\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\psi^{m}},{p_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,&=\,-\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}\,,\end{split}

(70)

combining the features of
Eqs. (
13
) and (
18
).
Crucially,
despite the covariance of the coordinates
(x^{\mu},p_{m},\psi^{m})
,
the Poisson brackets in Eq. (
70
)
do not manifest covariance.

Covariant Poisson Brackets

Meanwhile,
the isomorphism in Eq. (
29
)
implies that
the free-theory Poisson brackets in Eq. (
62
)
are reincarnated in local Lorentz frames as

\displaystyle{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\quad{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{D}\psi^{m}},{\bar{D}{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,.

(71)

Via the expansion in Eq. (
33
),
the covariant Poisson bracket in Eq. (
32
) describes

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{m}},{p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\text{cov}}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\psi^{m}},{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\text{cov}}\,&=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{m}},{p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\text{cov}}\,&=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\,,\end{split}

(72)

eliminating the non-tensorial objects in Eq. (
70
).

To elaborate,
the expansion in Eq. (
33
)
has described

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{m}},{p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\text{cov}}\,&=\,-\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{m}},{x^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\hskip 0.50003pt\,\Big(\,{-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{rs}(x)}\,\Big)\,\hskip 0.50003pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{s}},{p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\,,\end{split}

(73)

precisely reincarnating
the gymnastics in Eq. (
27
)
in Einstein’s elevator.

Dual Basis

One may desire to find the bivectors
\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}
and
\omega^{-1}
explicitly.
To this end,
note that

(\tilde{E}_{m},\partial/\partial p_{m},\partial/\partial\psi^{m},\partial/\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m})

serves as the
dual basis
to

(e^{m},Dp_{m},D\psi^{m},D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m})
,
where
\tilde{E}_{m}
is defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{r}\,=\,E^{\rho}{}_{r}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\rho}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}+p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}-\overleftarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}\,\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}+{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\overrightarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(74)

combining the features of Eqs. (
42
) and (
54
).
As a result, Eq. (
71
) is inverted as

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\,=\,\tilde{E}_{m}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}-i\,\overleftarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}\wedge\overrightarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}\,.

(75)

By Eq. (
30
),
it then follows that

\displaystyle\omega^{-1}\,=\,\tilde{E}_{m}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}-i\,\overleftarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}\wedge\overrightarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}-\frac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n}}\,.

(76)

The basis
(\tilde{E}_{m},\partial/\partial p_{m},\partial/\partial\psi^{m},\partial/\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m})
of vector fields
must be covariant,
since the basis
(e^{m},Dp_{m},D\psi^{m},D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m})
of one-forms
is covariant.
Therefore, Eq. (
76
)
is a manifestly gauge-covariant
representation of the interacting-theory Poisson bivector
\omega^{-1}
.

Ehresmann Connection

Again, it is left as an exercise to check that the vector field
\tilde{E}_{r}{\>\in\>}\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
in Eq. (
42
)
is covariant under local Lorentz and spacetime coordinate transformations.
It is exactly the horizontal lift of the spacetime vielbein

E_{r}\in\Gamma(T{\mathcal{M}})

with respect to the Levi-Civita connection
D
.
The Lie bracket
[\hskip 0.50003pt{\tilde{E}_{r}},{\tilde{E}_{s}}\hskip 0.50003pt]
computes to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Omega^{k}{}_{rs}(x)\,\tilde{E}_{k}+p_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n}}-\overleftarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}\,R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}+{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{n}{}_{mrs}(x)\,\overrightarrow{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}\,.\end{split}

(77)

Canonical Coordinates

Since
(\mathcal{A}_{2},\omega)
is a symplectic manifold,
the Darboux theorem applies.
Explicitly,
the canonical momentum can be identified as

\displaystyle{\kern 0.20004pt\mathtt{p}}_{\mu}\,=\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{mn}{}_{\mu}(x){\quad\implies\quad}\theta\,=\,{\kern 0.20004pt\mathtt{p}}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptd\psi^{m}\,.

(78)

Of course,
it is not a good idea to use canonical coordinates
(x^{\mu},{\kern 0.20004pt\mathtt{p}}_{\mu},\psi^{m})
.
In particular,
{\kern 0.20004pt\mathtt{p}}_{\mu}
in Eq. (
78
) is gauge-dependent
and is not a physical observable of the spinning particle:
one’s choice of the orthonormal frame
changes its value.

3.4
Ehresmann Frame

As before, let us end with a coordinate-free characterization of the ideas
unearthed in Secs.
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3
.

Covariantizer Reimagined

The astute reader may have noticed that
the covariantizer
\varrho
is essentially a
(1,1)
-tensor on a phase space
\mathcal{P}
,
mapping one-forms to one-forms:
to say,
“
\alpha_{I}\mapsto\alpha_{J}\hskip 1.00006pt\varrho^{J}{}_{I}
”
in an index notation.
In particular,
Eqs. (
35
), (
48
), and (
65
)
have mapped coordinate one-forms to non-coordinate one-forms,
like
\varrho(dX^{I})=\varrho^{I}{}_{J}\hskip 1.00006ptdX^{J}
.

With this observation,
we introduce a conceptually cleaner framework
that replaces the covariantizer
with a
non-coordinate coframe

\mathbf{e}^{A}=\mathbf{e}^{A}{}_{I}(X)\hskip 1.00006ptdX^{I}
on phase space.
Here,

I,J,K,L,\cdots

are coordinate indices of
\mathcal{P}
,
while

A,B,C,D,\cdots

are frame indices.
Both run through
(\dim\mathcal{P})
integers.
Its dual gives a
non-coordinate frame

\mathbf{E}_{A}=\mathbf{E}^{I}{}_{A}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{I}
.

The motivation for this refinement is to avoid some subtle or confusing aspects
of the covariantizer construction.
For instance,
our first version of
the covariant Poisson bracket in Eq. (
32
)
seems to yield uninsightful expressions when nested,
so a revision is desired.
Also, the notation
dx^{m}
in Eqs. (
50
) and (
67
)
can be a source of unnecessary confusion.
1
1

1

Its heuristic idea traces back to
an exploration on
multi-valued coordinates
à la Ref.
Kleinert:2008zzb
,
which may deviate from the standard differential geometry discourse.

Frame

A smooth manifold
\mathcal{P}

is
Framed

if it is equipped with
a set of vector fields
\mathbf{E}_{A}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})

for
A=1,2,{\cdots},(\dim\mathcal{P})

that is complete in the tangent space
T_{p}\mathcal{P}

for each point
p{\>\in\>}\mathcal{P}
.
In a framed smooth manifold
(\mathcal{P},\mathbf{E})
,
the
Coframe
refers to the set of one-forms

\mathbf{e}^{A}\in\Gamma(T^{*}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathcal{P})

that is dual to the frame:

\langle\mathbf{e}^{A},\mathbf{E}_{B}\rangle=\delta^{A}{}_{B}
.

The
Anholonomy Coefficients
of a frame
\mathbf{E}
on
\mathcal{P}

are
\mathbf{\Omega}^{A}{}_{BC}=\langle\mathbf{e}^{A},[\mathbf{E}_{B},\mathbf{E}_{C}]\rangle
.
If any of the them are nonzero,
then the frame
\mathbf{E}
said to be
Non-Coordinate
.

Ehresmann Frame

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a framed smooth manifold.
Let
E_{m}
be its frame,
where
m=1,2,{\cdots},(\dim{\mathcal{M}})
.
Suppose
\mathcal{P}
is a fiber bundle over
{\mathcal{M}}

equipped with an Ehresmann connection.
Then there exists a natural frame on
\mathcal{P}
,
which we call
the
Ehresmann Frame
.

Concretely,
construct a local coordinate system
(x^{\mu},v^{\alpha})
on
\mathcal{P}

based on a local patch on
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Here,
x^{\mu}
coordinatize the base
while
v^{\alpha}
coordinatize the fiber, so

\mu=1,{\cdots},(\dim{\mathcal{M}})
and
\alpha=(1{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\dim{\mathcal{M}}),{\cdots},(\dim\mathcal{P})
.
Let
\tilde{E}_{m}{\>\in\>}\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be the
the horizontal lift of
E_{m}\in\Gamma(T{\mathcal{M}})

with respect tor the Ehresmann connection.
The Ehresmann frame on
\mathcal{P}

is
\mathbf{E}_{A}=(\tilde{E}_{m},\partial/\partial v^{\alpha})
.
From the fact that

\tilde{E}_{m}
span the horizontal subspaces
while
\partial/\partial v^{\alpha}
span the vertical subspaces,
it should be clear that
\mathbf{E}_{A}
is nondegenerate,
i.e., provides a complete basis in
T_{p}\mathcal{P}
for each
p{\>\in\>}\mathcal{P}
.
The dual
\mathbf{e}^{A}
is called the
Ehresmann Coframe
.

Einstein’s Elevator via Ehresmann Frame

A
Free-Theory Structure
on
an even-dimensional smooth manifold
\mathcal{P}

is a constant symplectic matrix

\omega^{\circ}_{AB}

of rank
\dim\mathcal{P}
.
Let
(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}
be its inverse.
If
\mathcal{P}
is framed,
then it is an almost-symplectic manifold
via

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\mathbf{e}^{A}\wedge\mathbf{e}^{B}\,,\quad\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}\,\mathbf{E}_{A}\wedge\mathbf{E}_{B}\,.

(79)

We will be especially interested in the case when
\mathbf{E}
is an Ehresmann frame on a phase space.
Physically, it will
specify a
gauge-covariant yet non-coordinate basis
of vector fields.

In this geometrical language,
our explorations in
Secs.
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3

describe
the process of
endowing a proper covariant symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}

on this almost-symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega^{\bullet})

to generate a symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime})
.

Covariant Poisson Bracket Reimagined

Consequently, we realize that “covariant Poisson bracket”
means to examine the components of the Poisson bivector
in the non-coordinate basis.
That is, in a framed symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega,\mathbf{E})
we compute

\displaystyle\omega^{-1}(\mathbf{e}^{A},\mathbf{e}^{B})\,=\,(\omega^{-1})^{AB}\,.

(80)

Crucially, Eq. (
79
)
implies that the free-theory Poisson brackets are
recycled as

\displaystyle{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{\mathbf{e}^{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\ (\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}\,.

(81)

This is the symplectic geometry implementation of
Einstein’s elevator
,
in which case
the covariant symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}
encodes the
tidal (curvature) effects
.
With Eq. (
81
), Eq. (
30
) implies the expansion

\displaystyle{}\omega^{-1}(\mathbf{e}^{A},\mathbf{e}^{B})

(82)

\displaystyle{}\,=\,{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{\mathbf{e}^{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{\mathbf{e}^{C}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 0.24994pt\,\omega^{\prime}(\mathbf{E}_{A},\mathbf{E}_{B})\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{D}},{\mathbf{e}^{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle{}\,=\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}-(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AC}\,\omega^{\prime}_{AB}\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{DB}+(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AC}\,\omega^{\prime}_{CD}\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{DE}\,\omega^{\prime}_{EF}\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{FB}+\cdots\,,

where
\omega^{\prime}_{AB}:=\omega^{\prime}(\mathbf{E}_{A},\mathbf{E}_{B})
.
Clearly, these are the refined versions of Eqs. (
29
) and (
33
).

Eqs. (
80
), (
81
), and (
82
) will be utilized in actual calculations as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega^{-1}(\alpha,\beta)\\
&\,=\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\alpha},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{A}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,(\omega^{-1})^{AB}\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{B}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,,\\
&\,=\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\alpha},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{A}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{B}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle-\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\alpha},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{A}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AC}\,\omega^{\prime}_{CD}\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{DB}\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{B}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle+\cdots\,,\end{split}

(83)

where
\alpha,\beta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathcal{P})

will be covariant differentials.
Taking
\alpha{\>=\>}df
and
\beta{\>=\>}dg

for singlet-valued phase space functions
f,g\in C^{\infty}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathcal{P})
,
for instance,
will compute the Poisson bracket
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=\omega^{-1}(df,dg)

while manifesting covariance at intermediate steps.

Once the covariant Poisson brackets
\omega^{-1}(\mathbf{e}^{A},\mathbf{e}^{B})=(\omega^{-1})^{AB}

are fully known,
the ordinary Poisson brackets
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{X^{I}},{X^{J}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=(\omega^{-1})^{IJ}

between phase space coordinates
are completely determined by a simple basis change:

(\omega^{-1})^{IJ}=\mathbf{E}^{I}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{E}^{J}{}_{B}\hskip 1.00006pt(\omega^{-1})^{AB}
.
In this way, one fully characterizes the Poisson geometry
of phase spaces in a manifestly covariant fashion.

Covariant Jacobi Identity

Finally,
“covariant Jacobi identity”
means to examine the Schouten-Nijenhuis bracket

[\hskip 0.50003pt{\omega^{-1}},{\omega^{-1}}\hskip 0.50003pt]

in non-coordinate frames.

Appendix
A

explicates that
the vanishing of the Schouten-Nijenhuis bracket

[\hskip 0.50003pt{\omega^{-1}},{\omega^{-1}}\hskip 0.50003pt]

is equivalent to the closure
d\omega=0
,
yielding the Jacobi identity
{\mathrm{Jac}}(f,g,h)=0

when fully contracted with the exact differentials
df,dg,dh
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle J\,:=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt[\hskip 0.50003pt{\omega^{-1}},{\omega^{-1}}\hskip 0.50003pt]{\quad\implies\quad}{\mathrm{Jac}}(f,g,h)\,=\,J(df,dg,dh)\,.

(84)

As a generalization,
we compute
the Schouten-Nijenhuis bracket
in the non-coordinate basis.
Recalling its definition

schouten1940ueber
;
schouten1954differential
;
nijenhuis1955jacobi
,
we find that

J(\mathbf{e}^{A},\mathbf{e}^{B},\mathbf{e}^{C})=J^{ABC}

computes to

\displaystyle J^{ABC}\,=\,3\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\mathbf{E}_{D}[\,{(\omega^{-1})^{[AB|}}\,]\hskip 1.00006pt(\omega^{-1})^{D|C]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{[A|}{}_{EF}\hskip 1.00006pt(\omega^{-1})^{E|B|}\hskip 0.50003pt(\omega^{-1})^{F|C]}}\,\Big)\,,

(85)

which could be called the
Covariant Jacobiator
.
Therefore,
an equivalent statement of
the closure
d\omega=0
is

\displaystyle 3\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{-1}\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006ptd(\hskip 0.24994pt{\omega^{-1}(\mathbf{e}^{[A},\mathbf{e}^{B})})\hskip 1.00006pt,\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{e}^{C]}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,=\,3\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{\Omega}^{[A|}{}_{EF}\hskip 1.00006pt(\omega^{-1})^{E|B|}\hskip 0.50003pt(\omega^{-1})^{F|C]}\,.

(86)

Eq. (
86
) will be referred to as the
Covariant Jacobi Identity
.
Its left-hand side transcribes a typical combination,
while
its right-hand side represents apparent
obstructions
due to the anholonomy.
We may refer to these as
differential
and
algebraic
parts, respectively.
The involved nature of Eq. (
86
)
is a vivid demonstration of
how symplecticity becomes obscured
for the sake of manifest gauge covariance.

Note that Eq. (
86
)
can also be derived by using the identity in Eq. (
222
):

\displaystyle d\omega(\mathbf{E}_{A}^{\vphantom{\prime}},\mathbf{E}_{B}^{\vphantom{\prime}},\mathbf{E}_{C}^{\vphantom{\prime}})\,=\,3\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\mathbf{E}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{[A}[\,{\omega_{BC]}^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\,]+\omega_{[C|D}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{\Omega}^{D}{}_{|AB]}^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\,\Big)\,.

(87)

Covariant Geometry of Phase Spaces

In summary,
we conclude that
the covariant geometry of
a particle phase space
\mathcal{P}
,
fibering over spacetime to be equipped with an Ehresmann connection,
is defined by a triple of structures:
(a) free-theory structure
\omega^{\circ}
,
(b) Ehresmann frame
\mathbf{E}
,
and (c) covariant symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}
.
The goal of Section
4
is to
revisit our examples
with this refined formulation:
gauge theory, gravity, and gravity with spin.

4
Covariant Poisson Brackets

In this section, we provide a systematic analysis on the covariant symplectic geometry of particle phase spaces
and investigate how
covariant Poisson brackets obscure symplecticity.

4.1
Gauge Theory

Covariant Symplectic Geometry

The phase space

\mathcal{P}=T^{*}{\mathbb{M}}{\,\oplus\,}E

of the colored scalar particle
is characterized
by the covariant coordinates,

\displaystyle X^{I}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{x^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{i},\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,

(88)

the Ehresmann coframe/frame,

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}^{A}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{dx^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptdp_{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD\vartheta^{i},\hskip 1.00006ptD{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,\quad\mathbf{E}_{A}\,=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{\partial}_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta^{i}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(89)

the canonical free-theory structure such that

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\mathbf{e}^{A}\wedge\mathbf{e}^{B}\,=\,Dp_{m}\wedge dx^{m}+i\hskip 1.00006ptD{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\wedge D\vartheta^{i}\,,

(90)

and the proper covariant symplectic perturbation,

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,q_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,dx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{n}\,,

(91)

so
\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
Here, it is convenient to
formally regard
\vartheta^{i}
and
{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}
as independent variables.

Anholonomy Coefficients

The anholonomy coefficients are conveniently enumerated by

-d\mathbf{e}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{\Omega}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{e}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\mathbf{e}^{C}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{dx^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,0\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{dp_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,0\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,A^{i}{}_{jm}(x)\,\Big(\,{dx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\vartheta^{j}}\,\Big)-F^{i}{}_{jmn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{n}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,-A^{j}{}_{im}(x)\,\Big(\,{dx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}}\,\Big)+{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{j}{}_{imn}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{n}}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(92)

Otherwise, one can directly compute the Lie brackets
[\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{E}_{A}},{\mathbf{E}_{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt]
.

Covariant Poisson Brackets

Eq. (
90
) identifies an isomorphism to the free theory (pure-gauge dynamics),
while Eq. (
91
) encodes the curvature effect.
Hence, by straightforwardly recycling the free-theory Poisson brackets in Eq. (
10
),
the covariant expansion in Eq. (
82
) derives that
the non-vanishing components of
\omega^{-1}

in the Ehresmann basis are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\vartheta^{i}},{D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{i}{}_{j}\,,\\
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dp_{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,.\end{split}

(93)

Evidently, gauge covariance is manifested.

Covariant Jacobi Identity

Suppose we want to diagnose symplecticity
directly from the covariant Poisson brackets in Eq. (
93
).
To this end, we check the covariant Jacobi identity
in Eq. (
86
).
We compute its left-hand side and right-hand side separately.
It can be seen that
the nontrivial covariant Jacobiators are

J(dp_{m},dp_{n},D\vartheta^{i})

and

J(dp_{m},dp_{n},dp_{r})
.

First, we verify
J(dp_{m},dp_{n},D\vartheta^{i})=0
.
The differential part computes to

\displaystyle{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{D(q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x))}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{D\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\,=\,F^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{D\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-F^{i}{}_{jmn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}\,,

(94)

where we have used the fact that

d(q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x))=D(q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x))
.
Indeed,
the third line in Eq. (
92
) shows that
the algebraic part
exactly computes to
the same.

Second, we verify
J(dp_{m},dp_{n},dp_{r})=0
.
The differential part computes to

\displaystyle{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{D(q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x))}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{Dp_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\,=\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{s}F^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{s}},{Dp_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{r}F^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,,

(95)

up to total anti-symmetrization on the free indices
m,n,r
.
This vanishes by the Bianchi identity of non-abelian gauge fields:

\displaystyle D_{\smash{[r}\vphantom{\beta}}F^{a}{}_{\smash{mn]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,=\,0\,.

(96)

Indeed, the second line in Eq. (
92
) shows that
the algebraic part is exactly zero.

4.2
Gravity

Covariant Symplectic Geometry

The phase space

\mathcal{P}=T^{*}\hskip-1.00006pt{\mathcal{M}}

of the general-relativistic scalar particle
is characterized
by the covariant coordinates,

\displaystyle X^{I}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{x^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,

(97)

the Ehresmann coframe/frame,

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}^{A}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{D}p_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,\quad\mathbf{E}_{A}\,=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(98)

the canonical free-theory structure such that

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\mathbf{e}^{A}\wedge\mathbf{e}^{B}\,=\,\bar{D}p_{m}\wedge e^{m}\,,

(99)

and the proper covariant symplectic perturbation,

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\,e^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{n}\,,

(100)

so
\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
For full generality,
we assume the arbitrary Lorentz-valued connection
\bar{D}
.

Anholonomy Coefficients

The anholonomy coefficients are conveniently enumerated by

-d\mathbf{e}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{\Omega}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{e}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\mathbf{e}^{C}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,\Omega^{m}{}_{rs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{D}p_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,-\bar{\gamma}^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\bar{D}p_{n}}\,\Big)+p_{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{R}^{n}{}_{mrs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(101)

Covariant Poisson Brackets

Eq. (
99
) identifies an isomorphism to special relativity (pure-gauge dynamics)
in terms of the local orthonormal frame.
Hence, by straightforwardly recycling the free-theory Poisson brackets in Eq. (
2
),
the covariant expansion in Eq. (
82
) derives that
the non-vanishing components of
\omega^{-1}

in the Ehresmann basis are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{D}p_{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\,.\end{split}

(102)

Evidently, covariance is manifested.

Covariant Jacobi Identity

To diagnose symplecticity
directly from the covariant Poisson brackets in Eq. (
102
),
we check the covariant Jacobi identity
in Eq. (
86
).
It can be seen that
the nontrivial covariant Jacobiators are

J(\bar{D}p_{m},\bar{D}p_{n},e^{r})

and

J(\bar{D}p_{m},\bar{D}p_{n},\bar{D}p_{r})
.

A preliminary calculation is

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\,\xi^{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,=\,{}&{}\Big(\,{\bar{D}p_{k}\,\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}+p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{D}_{r}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\,e^{r}}\,\Big)\,\xi^{mn}\\[-0.96002pt]
{}&{}+p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\,\Big(\,{d\xi^{mn}+\bar{\gamma}^{m}{}_{l}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{ln}+\bar{\gamma}^{n}{}_{l}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{ml}}\,\Big)\,,\end{split}

(103)

where
\xi\in\Gamma(\wedge^{2}T{\mathcal{M}})

is an auxiliary bivector field on spacetime.
In turn,

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,=\,{}&{}\bar{D}p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)+p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{D}_{r}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ln}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{mr}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ml}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{nr}}\,\Big)\,e^{r}\,.\end{split}

(104)

Namely, one needs to be careful about the fact that

p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}
exhibits free indices
m,n
.
The occurrence of bare connection coefficients is unfortunate but seems unavoidable.

First, we verify
J(\bar{D}p_{m},\bar{D}p_{n},e^{r})=0
.
Using Eq. (
104
),
the differential part gives

\displaystyle{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{d(p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn})}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\,=\,\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{D}p_{k}},{e^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-\bar{T}^{r}{}_{mn}\,.

(105a)

Meanwhile,
The algebraic part computes to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{\Omega}^{(e^{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})(\tilde{E}_{n})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{m})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{n})({\partial/\partial}p_{r})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}+\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{n})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})({\partial/\partial}p_{r})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}=\,\Omega^{r}{}_{mn}+\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{mn}-\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{nm}\,.\end{split}

(105b)

Eqs. (
105a
) and (
105b
)
do agree, since

\bar{T}^{r}=de^{r}+\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{n}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{n}

encodes the identity

\bar{T}^{r}{}_{mn}+\Omega^{r}{}_{mn}+2\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{[mn]}=0
.

Second, we verify

J(\bar{D}p_{m},\bar{D}p_{n},\bar{D}p_{r})=0
.
Using Eq. (
104
),
the differential part gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{d(p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn})}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{D}p_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\\[-0.84009pt]
&=\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{D}_{r}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ln}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{mr}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ml}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{nr}}\,\Big)+\Big(\,{\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)\Big(\,{p_{l}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{kr}}\,\Big)\\[-0.84009pt]
&=\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{D}_{r}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ln}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{mr}-\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lm}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{nr}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lr}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)\,,\end{split}

(106a)

up to the index anti-symmetrization.
Meanwhile,
the algebraic part is

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})(\tilde{E}_{n})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}+\Big(\,{\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})({\partial/\partial}p_{l})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{l}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)

\displaystyle=\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{R}^{k}{}_{rmn}-\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{rm}\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ln}+\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{rn}\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lm}

\displaystyle=\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{R}^{k}{}_{rmn}-\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ln}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{rm}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lm}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{l}{}_{rn}}\,\Big)\,,

(106b)

up to the index anti-symmetrization.
Here, quadratic-in-
p
terms
are suppressed since

\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{({\partial/\partial}p_{k})({\partial/\partial}p_{l})}\vphantom{\beta}}=0
.
Consequently,
Eqs. (
106a
) and (
106
)
agree if

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{k}{}_{\smash{[rmn]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,=\,\bar{D}_{\smash{[r}\vphantom{\beta}}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{\smash{mn]}\vphantom{\beta}}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{\smash{l[r}\vphantom{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{\smash{mn]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,,

(107)

as the bare connection terms cancel upon the total anti-symmetrization.
Eq. (
107
) is the first Bianchi identity in Riemann-Cartan geometry,

R^{k}{}_{m}\wedge e^{m}=\bar{D}\bar{D}e^{k}=\bar{D}T^{k}
.
Equivalently,
it can be shown that
Eq. (
107
)
arises from

[\hskip 0.50003pt{[\hskip 0.50003pt{E_{[m}},{E_{n\vphantom{]}}}\hskip 0.50003pt]},{E_{r]}}\hskip 0.50003pt]=0
,
which precisely is

the
Bianchi identity
in Eq. (
96
)
for diffeomorphism gauge theory:

\partial_{[r}F^{\mu}{}_{mn]}+[\hskip 0.50003pt{A_{[r}},{F{}_{mn]}}\hskip 0.50003pt]^{\mu}=0
.

Despite the simplicity of Eq. (
102
),
the check of symplecticity is
substantially complicated.
Yet, adopting the Levi-Civita connection

\bar{D}=D

will simplify the process.

4.3
Gravity with Spin

Covariant Symplectic Geometry

The phase space

\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{A}_{2}=(T^{*}{\,\oplus\,}\Pi T^{\mathbb{C}})\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathcal{M}}

of the general-relativistic spinning particle
is characterized
by the covariant coordinates,

\displaystyle X^{I}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{x^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{m},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,

(108)

the Ehresmann coframe/frame,

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}^{A}\,=\,\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptDp_{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD\psi^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,\quad\mathbf{E}_{A}\,=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(109)

the canonical free-theory structure such that

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\mathbf{e}^{A}\wedge\mathbf{e}^{B}\,=\,Dp_{m}\wedge e^{m}+i\hskip 1.00006ptD{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\wedge D\psi^{m}\,,

(110)

and the proper covariant symplectic perturbation,

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\,e^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{n}\,,

(111)

so
\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
Here, it is convenient to
formally treat
\psi^{m}
and
{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}
as independent variables.

Anholonomy Coefficients

The anholonomy coefficients are conveniently enumerated by

-d\mathbf{e}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{\Omega}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{e}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\mathbf{e}^{C}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,\Omega^{m}{}_{rs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\bar{D}p_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,-\gamma^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}Dp_{n}}\,\Big)+p_{n}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{n}{}_{mrs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D\psi^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\psi^{n}}\,\Big)-R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,-\gamma^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}}\,\Big)+{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{n}{}_{mrs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(112)

Eq. (
112
) combines the features of Eqs. (
92
) and (
101
).

Covariant Poisson Brackets

Eq. (
110
) identifies an isomorphism to special relativity (pure-gauge dynamics),
while Eq. (
111
) encodes the tidal effect.
Hence, by straightforwardly recycling the free-theory Poisson brackets in Eq. (
62
),
the covariant expansion in Eq. (
82
) derives that
the non-vanishing components of
\omega^{-1}

in the Ehresmann basis are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\[-1.20007pt]
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\psi^{m}},{D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\[-1.20007pt]
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\,.\end{split}

(113)

Evidently, covariance is manifested.

Covariant Jacobi Identity

To diagnose symplecticity
directly from the covariant Poisson brackets in Eq. (
113
),
we check the covariant Jacobi identity
in Eq. (
86
).
It can be seen that
the nontrivial covariant Jacobiators are

J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},e^{r})
,

J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},D\psi^{r})
,
and

J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},Dp_{r})
.
The vanishing of the first one is easy
by
\Omega^{k}{}_{mn\vphantom{]}}+2\gamma^{k}{}_{[mn]}=0
.

A preliminary calculation is

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}\,\xi^{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,=\,{}&{}\smash{\Big(\,{DS_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}+S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptD_{r}R^{kl}{}_{mn}\,e^{r}}\,\Big)\,\xi^{mn}}\\[-0.96002pt]
{}&{}+S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}\,\Big(\,{d\xi^{mn}+\gamma^{m}{}_{s}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{sn}+\gamma^{n}{}_{s}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{ms}}\,\Big)\,,\end{split}

(114)

where
\xi\in\Gamma(\wedge^{2}T{\mathcal{M}})

is an auxiliary bivector field on spacetime.
In turn,

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,=\,{}&{}\bar{D}S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{kl}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)+S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{D_{r}R^{kl}{}_{mn}+R^{kl}{}_{sn}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{s}{}_{mr}+R^{kl}{}_{ms}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{s}{}_{nr}}\,\Big)\,e^{r}\,.\end{split}

(115)

Namely, one needs to be careful about the fact that

S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}
exhibits free indices
m,n
.
The occurrence of bare connection coefficients is unfortunate but seems unavoidable.

First, we verify
J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},D\psi^{r})=0
.
Using Eq. (
115
),
the differential part gives

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\>{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{d(S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{mn})}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{D\psi^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,R^{kl}{}_{mn}\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{DS_{kl}},{D\psi^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-R^{r}{}_{smn}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{s}\,.

(116a)

This calculation combines the features of both Eqs. (
94
) and (
105a
).
Indeed,
the algebraic part computes to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{\Omega}^{(D\psi^{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})(\tilde{E}_{n})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}=\,-R^{r}{}_{smn}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{s}\,,\end{split}

(116b)

reproducing Eq. (
116a
).
This calculation is rather a mere revival of the gauge theory story.

Second, we verify

J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},Dp_{r})=0
.
Using Eq. (
115
),
the differential part gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\tfrac{1}{2}\>{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\Big(\,{\hskip 0.50003pt{d(S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x))}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 0.24994pt,\hskip 1.00006pt{Dp_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\,\Big)}\\[-0.84009pt]
&=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{kl}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{D_{r}R^{kl}{}_{mn}+R^{kl}{}_{sn}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{s}{}_{mr}-R^{kl}{}_{sm}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{s}{}_{nr}}\,\Big)\,,\end{split}

(117a)

up to the index anti-symmetrization.
This calculation is simpler than Eq. (
106a
),
as we have set the torsion to zero
to focus on the curvature effects.
Meanwhile,
the algebraic part computes to

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})(\tilde{E}_{n})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}+\Big(\,{\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{(\tilde{E}_{m})({\partial/\partial}p_{s})}\vphantom{0^{0}}}\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{s}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)

\displaystyle=\,p_{s}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{s}{}_{rmn}-\bar{\gamma}^{s}{}_{rm}\,\Big(\,{-\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{sn}}\,\Big)+\bar{\gamma}^{s}{}_{rn}\,\Big(\,{-\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{kl}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{kl}{}_{sm}}\,\Big)

\displaystyle=\,p_{s}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{s}{}_{rmn}-\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{kl}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{-R^{kl}{}_{sn}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{s}{}_{rm}+R^{kl}{}_{sm}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{s}{}_{rn}}\,\Big)\,,

(117b)

up to the index anti-symmetrization.
Here, quadratic-in-
S
terms
are suppressed since

\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\bar{D}p_{r})}{}_{\smash{({\partial/\partial}p_{k})({\partial/\partial}p_{l})}\vphantom{\beta}}=0
.
This calculation is similar to Eq. (
106
).
By equating
Eqs. (
117a
) and (
117
),
we find

\displaystyle R^{s}{}_{\smash{[rmn]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,=\,0\,,\quad D_{\smash{[r}\vphantom{\beta}}R^{kl}{}_{\smash{mn]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,=\,0\,,

(118)

as the bare connection terms cancel upon the total anti-symmetrization of indices.
Eq. (
118
)
derives the first and second Bianchi identities in Riemannian geometry,

R^{s}{}_{r}\wedge e^{r}=DDe^{s}=0

and

DR^{kl}=0
.
In light of our previous explorations in Sections
4.1
and
4.2
,
the first Bianchi identity
will be attributed to the diffeomorphism charge (momentum),
tracing back to Eq. (
107
),
while
the second Bianchi identity
will be attributed to the local Lorentz charge (spin),
paralleling Eq. (
96
).

Despite the simplicity of
the covariant Poisson brackets in
Eq. (
113
),
the check of symplecticity is
considerably complicated.

4.4
Symplectic Vielbein

Before moving on, let us introduce a useful and interesting idea:

symplectic vielbein formalism
.

Local Flatness in Symplectic Geometry

In general relativity,
the vielbein formalism
concerns a “square root” of the metric:

g_{\mu\nu}(x)=\eta_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}{}_{\nu}(x)
.
Here,
\eta_{mn}
is a constant symmetric matrix.
As a result, the vielbein description of the curved geometry
fully embraces
the
local flatness
in Riemannian geometry.

We propose to implement the vielbein formalism in symplectic geometry
by the formula

\displaystyle\omega_{IJ}(X)\,=\,\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}{}_{I}(X)\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{}_{J}(X)\,,

(119)

which concerns a “square root” of the symplectic form.
Here,
\omega^{\circ}_{AB}
is a constant anti-symmetric matrix of full rank:
the free-theory structure.

The astute reader will sense that
Eq. (
119
) is precisely a compromise between manifest covariance and symplecticity.
In symplectic geometry,
the Darboux theorem
darboux1882probleme

states
global flatness
in a sense,
meaning that
any symplectic form
\omega

can be flawlessly “ironed out”
to exhibit constant components (
\pm 1
and
0
)
within a finite region of nonzero extension.
This is by the use of canonical coordinates of course,
but the central lesson of Section
2

has been that canonical coordinates are abandoned for manifest covariance.
The compromise is
local flatness
,
which means to achieve Darboux
only within the infinitesimal vicinity of each point.
In this way, manifest symplecticity is obstructed
yet to a slightly milder degree.

To be explicit,
recall the well-known result in Riemannian geometry
that the normal coordinates
y^{m}
adapted to an orthonormal frame
describe

\displaystyle g\,=\,\bigg(\,{\eta_{mn}+\frac{1}{3}\,R_{mrsn}(y{\>=\>}0)\,y^{r}y^{s}+{\mathcal{O}}(y^{3})}\,\bigg)\,dy^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt\odot\hskip 1.00006pt}dy^{n}\,.

(120)

By
local
flatness,
it means that the bracketed combination in Eq. (
120
)
inevitably deviates from the flat metric
\eta_{mn}

for
y\neq 0
,
in the presence of Riemannian curvature.
The vielbein describes the Jacobian factor
between generic and normal coordinates
at
y=0
.

The Darboux theorem asserts that,
around any point in a symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega)
,
there exist coordinates
{\scalebox{1.25}[1.02]{$\mathtt{Y}$}}^{I}

such that

\displaystyle\omega\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>\omega^{\circ}_{IJ}\,d{\scalebox{1.25}[1.02]{$\mathtt{Y}$}}^{I}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\scalebox{1.25}[1.02]{$\mathtt{Y}$}}^{J}

(121)

at points with any value of
{\scalebox{1.25}[1.02]{$\mathtt{Y}$}}^{I}
.
It can be shown that a weaker statement holds:
there exist normal coordinates
Y^{A}

around any point in a symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega)

such that

\displaystyle\omega\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>\Big(\,{\omega^{\circ}_{AB}+{\mathcal{O}}(Y^{2})}\,\Big)\,dY^{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dY^{B}\,.

(122)

The symplectic vielbein describes the Jacobian factor
between generic and normal coordinates
at
Y=0
.

Having described the significance of the idea
and sketched its intuition,
we provide a precise formalization below.

Symplectic Vielbein

Let
(\mathcal{P},\omega)
be an almost-symplectic manifold.
A
Locally Canonical Coframe

is a set of one-forms

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathcal{P})

such that

\displaystyle\omega\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\wedge\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}

(123)

for a free-theory structure
\omega^{\circ}
.
This will be also referred to as a
Symplectic Vielbein
.

The dual of
\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}

is the set of vector fields

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{A}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})

such that

\langle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A},\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{B}\rangle=\delta^{A}{}_{B}
,
which will be called a
Locally Canonical Frame
.
It should be clear that

\displaystyle\omega^{-1}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{A}\wedge\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{B}\,.

(124)

The anholonomy coefficients of the locally canonical frame
are
\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{A}{}_{BC}=\langle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A},[\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{B},\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{C}]\rangle
.

In the symplectic vielbein formalism,
the frame indices
A,B,C,D,\cdots

may be raised and lowered by
the free-theory structure as

\displaystyle\alpha^{A}\,=\,(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}\hskip 1.00006pt\alpha_{B}{\quad\iff\quad}\alpha_{A}\,=\,\omega^{\circ}_{AB}\hskip 1.00006pt\alpha^{B}\,.

(125)

Covariant Jacobi Identity

Recall the covariant Jacobiator in Eq. (
85
),
which examined the Ehresmann frame components of the trivector

J=-\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt[\hskip 0.50003pt{\omega^{-1}},{\omega^{-1}}\hskip 0.50003pt]
.
In the symplectic vielbein formalism,
one instead examines the components of this trivector
in the locally canonical frame.
Since
\omega^{-1}(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A},\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B})=(\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1})^{AB}

are just constants,
one finds

\displaystyle J^{ABC}\,=\,-3\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{[ABC]}\,.

(126)

In this way, the verification of symplecticity
becomes relatively simpler
as compared to the Ehresmann frame formalism in Section
3.4
.
This supports the intuition that
the symplectic vielbein is a compromise
between manifest covariance and symplecticity.
As a result, an equivalent statement of the closure
d\omega=0
is

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{ABC}+\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{BCA}+\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{CAB}\,=\,0\,,

(127)

which is the covariant Jacobi identity in the symplectic vielbein formalism.

Gauge Theory, Revisited

Let us revisit Section
4.1

with the symplectic vielbein formalism.
The gauge-covariant
and
locally canonical coframe and frame are

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\Big(\,{dx^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptdp_{m}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{n},\hskip 1.00006ptD\vartheta^{i},\hskip 1.00006ptD{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\,\Big)\,,

(128a)

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{\partial}_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta^{i}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(128b)

Together with the covariant coordinates
(x^{m},p_{m},\vartheta^{i},{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i})
,
Eq. (
128
)
provides a succinct summary
of the exact phase space geometry

({\mathcal{P}{\>=\>}T^{*}{\mathbb{M}}{\,\oplus\,}E}\hskip 0.50003pt,\hskip 1.00006pt\omega)
.

The anholonomy coefficients
are read off from

-d\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{C}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{dx^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,0\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,\tfrac{i}{2}\,D({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{j})\hskip 1.00006pt\wedge F^{i}{}_{jmn}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{m}F^{a}{}_{rs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{s}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,A^{i}{}_{jm}(x)\,\Big(\,{dx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\vartheta^{j}}\,\Big)-F^{i}{}_{jmn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{n}}\,\Big)\,,\\
-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,&=\,-A^{j}{}_{im}(x)\,\Big(\,{dx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}}\,\Big)+{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{j}{}_{imn}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{n}}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(129)

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}:=dp_{m}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{n}
.
The Bianchi identity has simplified the second line.

There are two sorts of
nontrivial covariant Jacobiators.
The first kind vanishes easily:

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{D\vartheta^{i}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(D\vartheta^{i})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}+i\,\Big(\,{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m})}{}_{({\partial/\partial}{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\Big(\,{F^{i}{}_{jmn}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}}\,\Big)+i\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{i}{2}\,F^{i}{}_{jmn}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(130)

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m}:=\tilde{\partial}_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt{\partial/\partial}p_{n}
.
The second kind vanishes by the Bianchi identity:

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})}\,,\\
\,&=\,-\tfrac{3}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{\smash{[m}\vphantom{\beta}}F^{a}{}_{\smash{rs]}\vphantom{\beta}}\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(131)

Gravity, Revisited

Let us revisit Section
4.3

with the symplectic vielbein formalism.
The covariant
and
locally canonical coframe and frame are

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\Big(\,{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{D}p_{m}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}}\,\Big)\,,

(132a)

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(132b)

Together with the covariant coordinates
(x^{\mu},p_{m})
,
Eq. (
132
)
provides a succinct summary
of the exact phase space geometry

({\mathcal{P}{\>=\>}T^{*}\hskip-1.00006pt{\mathcal{M}}}\hskip 0.50003pt,\hskip 1.00006pt\omega)
.

The anholonomy coefficients
are read off from

-d\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{C}
:

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,\Omega^{m}{}_{rs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,

(133)

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} &{-\Big(\,{\bar{\gamma}^{n}{}_{mr}(x)+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bar{T}^{n}{}_{mr}(x)}\,\Big)}\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}}\,\Big)+p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big(\,{\bar{R}^{k}{}_{mrs}(x)+\Xi^{k}{}_{mrs}(x)}\,\Big)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,.\end{aligned}

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}:=\bar{D}p_{m}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}
.
Also,
\Xi^{k}{}_{mrs}(x)=-\Xi^{k}{}_{msr}(x)

denotes a tensor field on
{\mathcal{M}}
.
It arises by
expanding

\bar{D}(\frac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mr}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r})

and then replacing
\bar{D}p_{k}
with
\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\,p_{l}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{ks}(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{s}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Xi^{k}{}_{mrs}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}\,&=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lr}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{ms}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}+\bar{D}\Big(\,{\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mr}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\Big(\,{\bar{D}_{r}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{ms}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lm}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{rs}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bar{T}^{k}{}_{lr}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{ms}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}\,.\end{split}

(134)

There are two sorts of
nontrivial covariant Jacobiators.
The first kind vanishes by the identity

\bar{T}^{r}{}_{mn}+\Omega^{r}{}_{mn}+2\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{[mn]}=0

used earlier in Section
4.2
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{r}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(e^{r})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}+\Big(\,{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})({\partial/\partial}p_{r})}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\Omega^{r}{}_{mn}+2\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\gamma}^{r}{}_{\smash{[mn]}\vphantom{\beta}}(x)+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bar{T}^{r}{}_{\smash{mn}\vphantom{\beta}}}\,\Big)\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(135)

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m}:=\tilde{E}_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\,p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt{\partial/\partial}p_{n}
.
The second kind vanishes by the Bianchi identity in Eq. (
107
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})}\,,\\
\,&=\,-3\hskip 1.00006ptp_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big(\,{\bar{R}^{k}{}_{[rmn]}(x)+\Xi^{k}{}_{[rmn]}(x)}\,\Big)\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(136)

To see this, read off
\Xi^{k}{}_{mrs}
from Eq. (
134
) to find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Xi^{k}{}_{[mrs]}\,=\,-\bar{D}_{[m}\bar{T}^{k}{}_{rs]}-\bar{T}^{k}{}_{l[m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{l}{}_{rs]}\,=\,-\bar{R}^{k}{}_{[mrs]}\,.\end{split}

(137)

Gravity with Spin, Revisited

Let us revisit Section
4.3

with the symplectic vielbein formalism.
The gauge-covariant
and
locally canonical coframe and frame are

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\Big(\,{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptDp_{m}+\tfrac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{n},\hskip 1.00006ptD\psi^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\,\Big)\,,

(138a)

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{\mathbf{E}}}{}_{A}\,

\displaystyle=\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m}+\frac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}\vphantom{{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}^{0}}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(138b)

Together with the covariant coordinates
(x^{m},p_{m},\psi^{m},{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m})
,
Eq. (
138
)
provides a succinct summary
of the exact phase space geometry

({\mathcal{P}{\>=\>}\mathcal{A}_{2}=(T^{*}{\,\oplus\,}\Pi T^{\mathbb{C}}){\mathcal{M}}}\hskip 0.50003pt,\hskip 1.00006pt\omega)
.

The anholonomy coefficients
are read off from

-d\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{A}{}_{BC}\hskip 1.00006pt\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{C}
:

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,\Omega^{m}{}_{rs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,

(139)

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,\tfrac{i}{2}\,D({\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\psi^{l})\hskip 1.00006pt\wedge R^{k}{}_{lmn}(x)\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}+\Big(\,{p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{R}^{k}{}_{mrs}(x)-\tfrac{1}{4}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{m}R^{kl}{}_{rs}(x)}\,\Big)\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D\psi^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\psi^{n}}\,\Big)-R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{n}\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,,

\displaystyle-d\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,

\displaystyle=\,-\gamma^{n}{}_{mr}(x)\,\Big(\,{e^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}}\,\Big)+{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{n}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{n}{}_{mrs}(x)\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}}\,\Big)\,.

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}:=Dp_{m}+\tfrac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}
.
The second Bianchi identity has been used to simplify the second line a bit.

There are three sorts of
nontrivial covariant Jacobiators.
The first kind vanishes easily:

J(Dp_{m},Dp_{n},e^{r})=0
.
The second kind
vanishes by mimicking Eq. (
130
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{D\psi^{r}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(D\psi^{r})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}+i\,\Big(\,{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m})}{}_{({\partial/\partial}\psi^{r})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)\,,\\[-1.20007pt]
\,&=\,\Big(\,{R^{r}{}_{smn}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{s}}\,\Big)+i\,\Big(\,{\tfrac{i}{2}\,R^{r}{}_{smn}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi^{s}-(m{\hskip 1.00006pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 1.00006pt}n)}\,\Big)\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(140)

Here,

\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m}:=\tilde{E}_{m}+\frac{1}{4}\,S_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{kl}{}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt{\partial/\partial}p_{n}
.
The third kind vanishes by
the first and second Bianchi identities of Riemannian geometry,
shown in Eq. (
118
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}J\hskip 0.24994pt\big(\hskip 0.50003pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n}},\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big)\,&=\,-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{r})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{m})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{n})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})}-\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}^{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{p}}}{}_{n})}{}_{(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{r})(\smash{\acute{\mathbf{X}}}{}_{m})}\,,\\[-1.20007pt]
\,&=\,-3\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{k}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{R}^{k}{}_{[mrs]}(x)-\tfrac{1}{4}\hskip 1.00006ptS_{kl}\hskip 0.50003ptD_{[m}R^{kl}{}_{rs]}(x)}\,\Big)\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(141)

Remarks

Arguably,
the process of checking covariant Jacobi identities
is simpler
in the symplectic vielbein formalism,
as the
differential part
is made to vanish by construction.
Especially,
the preliminary calculations
in Sections
4.2
and
4.3
,
which employed the auxiliary bivector field
\xi

and produced extra bare connection terms,
could be entirely omitted.

On a related note,
it is interesting to compare between
the (covariant) Jacobi identities in
(a) the ordinary coordinate frame formalism,
(b) the Ehresmann frame formalism,
and
(c) the symplectic vielbein formalism.
In case (a), information is stored solely on the
differential part.
In case (b), both
the differential and algebraic parts
are used.
In case (c), information is stored solely on the
algebraic part.

Lastly, the simplest test of symplecticity is of course
the direct examination of
d\omega=0
.
In the symplectic vielbein formalism,
it is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle d\omega\,=\,d\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}\wedge\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}_{A}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\>\smash{\acute{\mathbf{\Omega}}}{}_{ABC}\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{C}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{3!}\>J_{ABC}\,\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{B}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\smash{\acute{\mathbf{e}}}{}^{C}\,.

(142)

It is also left as an easy exercise to compute
d\omega
for our example systems,
in which case one generates the Bianchi identities in Eqs. (
96
), (
107
), and (
118
)
immediately.

5
Covariant Equations of Motion

Finally, we present the most important application of the covariant Poisson bracket:
manifestly covariant derivations of classical equations of motion.

The geometrical statement of the Hamiltonian equations of motion
is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\,=\,{\mathbbm{X}\kern-0.29999pt}_{{\hskip 0.2048pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.2048pt}(\tau)}\,.

(143)

The
Time-Evolution Vector Field

d/d\tau{\>\in\>}\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})

defines the time evolution on
\mathcal{P}
.
The
Time-Evolution Generator

{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)
is a one-parameter family of functions on
\mathcal{P}
,
where
\tau
is the time parameter.
The coordinate-based approach to the Hamiltonian equations of motion
contracts the each side of Eq. (
143
)
with coordinate differentials:

\displaystyle\dot{X}^{I}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptdX^{I}\,=\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{dX^{I}},\hskip 0.50003pt{{\mathbbm{X}\kern-0.29999pt}_{{\hskip 0.2048pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.2048pt}(\tau)}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{X^{I}},{{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dX^{I}},{d{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,.

(144)

Even if the coordinates
X^{I}
are gauge-covariant and non-canonical,
however, Eq. (
144
) does not manifest covariance
because the bare time derivative
\dot{X}^{I}
will not be covariant.

Instead of following this textbook path,
we propose to contract each side of Eq. (
143
)
with the gauge-covariant yet non-coordinate coframe,
i.e., the Ehresmann coframe:

\displaystyle\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathbf{e}^{A}\,=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{d{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{\mathbf{e}^{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\mathbf{E}_{B}[\,{{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)}\,]\,.

(145)

Since
{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)
must be a gauge singlet,
Eq. (
145
) determines the classical equations of motion
in a manifestly covariant way
via the covariant Poisson brackets
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathbf{e}^{A}},{\mathbf{e}^{B}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}
.
We refer to Eq. (
145
) as
the
Covariant Hamiltonian Equations Of Motion
.

5.1
Gauge Theory

Covariant Poisson Geometry

The colored scalar particle
is a Hamiltonian system
whose phase space
\mathcal{P}=T^{*}{\mathbb{M}}{\,\oplus\,}E

describes physical variables

x^{m}
(position),

p_{m}
(momentum),
and

q_{a}
(color charge).
In the presence of a background non-abelian gauge field,
their covariant Poisson brackets are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{Dq_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,q_{c}\hskip 1.00006ptf^{c}{}_{ab}\,,\\
{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dp_{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,.\end{split}

(146)

Covariant Equations of Motion

Contracting
the Hamiltonian vector field of
\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2}

with the covariant one-forms
dx^{m}
,
dp_{m}
, and
Dq_{a}
,
it is easily found that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{x}^{m}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,p^{m}\,,\\
\dot{p}_{m}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptdp_{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dp_{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,q_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptF^{a}{}_{mn}(x)\,p^{n}\,,\\
\frac{Dq_{a}}{d\tau}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptDq_{a}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,0\,,\end{split}

(147)

where we have used
d(\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2})=dp_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{m}
.
Eq. (
147
) directly reproduces the Wong’s equations
wong1970field

in a manifestly covariant fashion.
Compare this with the textbook derivation in Eq. (
14
)
which used the ordinary Poisson bracket.

To elaborate, the time-evolution generator for the colored scalar particle is

\displaystyle{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)\,=\,\frac{\kappa^{0}(\tau)}{2}\,p^{2}+\kappa^{1}(\tau)\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}-w}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,,

(148)

where
the Lagrange multipliers

\kappa^{0}(\tau)
and
\kappa^{1}(\tau)
are sole functions of
\tau
,
and
w
is a constant;
recall the brief discussion in Section
2.2
.
By worldline parametrization, we are free to choose
\kappa^{0}(\tau)=1
.
On the other hand,
\kappa^{1}(\tau)
can be simply left agnostic in deriving Eq. (
147
).
From the complete covariant Poisson brackets in Eq. (
93
),
it is easy to see that
the
\mathrm{U}(1)
constraint
{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}-w
commutes with
the color charge in the covariant sense:
2
2

2

In the covariantizer mode of thinking,
we recycle the known Poisson bracket in the free theory
to immediately recognize

\omega^{-1}(Dq_{a},D({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}))=\omega^{\bullet}{}^{-1}(Dq_{a},D({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}))=\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1}(dq_{a},d({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}))=\{\hskip 0.50003pt{q_{a}},{{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{d({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i})}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{D({\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 0.50003pt\vartheta^{i})}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\\
\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{i}+{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\,\hskip 0.50003pt{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dq_{a}},{D\vartheta^{i}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\\
\,&=\,{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}\hskip 1.00006pt(t_{a})^{j}{}_{i}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{i}-{\bar{\vartheta}}_{i}\hskip 1.00006pt(t_{a})^{i}{}_{j}\hskip 1.00006pt\vartheta^{j}\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(149)

If desired, one can also easily derive the covariant equations of motion for
\vartheta^{i}
:

\displaystyle\frac{D\vartheta^{i}}{d\tau}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptD\vartheta^{i}\,

\displaystyle=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\vartheta^{i}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}+\kappa^{1}\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\vartheta^{i}},{D{\bar{\vartheta}}_{j}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\vartheta^{j}\,=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\kappa^{1}\,\vartheta^{i}\,.

(150)

5.2
Gravity

Covariant Poisson Geometry

The general-relativistic scalar particle
is a Hamiltonian system
whose phase space
\mathcal{P}=T^{*}\hskip-1.00006pt{\mathcal{M}}

describes physical variables

x^{\mu}
(position)
and

p_{m}
(momentum).
In the gravitational background,
their covariant Poisson brackets are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{e^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,,\quad{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\quad{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,,\end{split}

(151)

where we have employed the Levi-Civita connection
D
.

Covariant Equations of Motion

Contracting
the Hamiltonian vector field of
\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2}

with the covariant one-forms
e^{m}
and
Dp_{m}
,
it is easily found that

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\,\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,p^{m}\,,\\
\frac{Dp_{m}}{d\tau}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptDp_{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,0\,,\end{split}

(152)

where we have used
d(\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2})=D(\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2})=Dp_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{m}
.
Obviously,
Eq. (
152
) states the geodesic equation
in the first-order form.
Switching to the coordinate frame immediately yields

\displaystyle\frac{D}{d\tau}\,\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,0{\quad\iff\quad}\ddot{x}^{\mu}+\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)\,\dot{x}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,=\,0\,,

(153)

where
\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)
are the Christoffel symbols.
Compare this with the textbook derivation
sketched around Eq. (
16
),
which uses the ordinary Poisson bracket
and generates partial derivatives of the inverse metric.
The physical meaning is also clear—Eq. (
152
) is a direct lift of the free-theory equations of motion
via Einstein’s elevator (local inertial frame):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,p^{m}\,,\\
\dot{p}_{m}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006ptdp_{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{\circ}{}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dp_{m}},{dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,0\,.\end{split}

(154)

To elaborate, the time-evolution generator for the general-relativistic scalar particle is

\displaystyle{\hskip 0.24994pt\mathsf{H}\hskip 0.24994pt}(\tau)\,=\,\frac{\kappa^{0}(\tau)}{2}\,p^{2}\,,

(155)

where
p^{2}=\eta^{mn}\hskip 0.50003ptp_{m}\hskip 0.24994ptp_{n}
.
Without loss of generality,
we adopt
an affine parameter:
\kappa^{0}=1
.

With Generic Connection

More generally, we can also repeat the above derivation with an arbitrary Lorentz-valued connection
\bar{D}
.
The covariant Poisson brackets are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{e^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,,\quad{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\quad{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{D}p_{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\,.\end{split}

(156)

Contracting
the Hamiltonian vector field of
\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2}

with the covariant one-forms
e^{m}
and
\bar{D}p_{m}
,
it is easily found that

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\,\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,p^{m}\,,\\
\frac{\bar{D}p_{m}}{d\tau}\,=\,\iota_{d/d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{D}p_{m}\,&=\,{\omega^{-1}\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\bar{D}p_{m}},{\bar{D}p_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p^{n}\,=\,p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\,p^{n}\,,\end{split}

(157)

where we have used
d(\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2})=\bar{D}(\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2})=\bar{D}p_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{m}
.
Eq. (
157
) still describes the geodesic equation,
regardless of one’s choice of
\bar{D}
.

To see this,
let
\delta\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}:=\bar{\gamma}^{m}{}_{nr}-\gamma^{m}{}_{nr}

describe the difference between the arbitrary connection
\bar{D}

and the Levi-Civita connection
D
,
which is a
tensor
.
Then the second equation in Eq. (
157
) boils down to

\displaystyle\begin{split}0\,&=\,\frac{\bar{D}p_{m}}{d\tau}-p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}\,p^{n}\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{Dp_{m}}{d\tau}-p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\delta\gamma^{k}{}_{mn}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)\,p^{n}\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{Dp_{m}}{d\tau}-p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{2\hskip 1.00006pt\delta\gamma^{k}{}_{[mn]}+\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}}\,\Big)\,p^{n}\,.\end{split}

(158)

Meanwhile, from the torsion-free condition of the Levi-Civita connection,
one finds

\displaystyle 0\,=\,De^{k}\,=\,\bar{D}e^{k}-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\delta\gamma^{k}{}_{nm}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\wedge e^{n}\,=\,\bar{T}^{k}+\delta\gamma^{k}{}_{[mn]}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}\wedge e^{n}\,,

(159)

which implies

\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}+2\hskip 1.00006pt\delta\gamma^{k}{}_{[mn]}=0
.
Therefore, the actual contents of
Eqs. (
152
) and (
157
)
are the same.

Eq. (
157
)
provides an exotic yet intriguing reformulation of the geodesic equation
in the form of the Lorentz force law,
where
p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}
serves as the “field strength.”
Namely,

p_{k}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{T}^{k}{}_{mn}(x)\,p^{n}
in Eq. (
157
)
is the
gravitational force

represented as a Lorentz-type force.
It will be interesting to assess this fact
from the angle of gravito-electromagnetism
wald2010general
.
In the case of Born-Infeld theory as a diffeomorphism-valued gauge theory,
this Lorentz force interpretation is not a mere analogy
but an exact, literal statement,
due to the discussion in Section
3.2
.

The reason why such Lorentz force formulation arises
should be clear:
we have fixed the Hamiltonian constraint to the free-theory form
as a full implementation of the Souriau philosophy,
so time evolution must describe successive Lorentz transformations
on the orthonormal-frame momentum
p_{m}
:
the momentum-squared
p^{2}
is dynamically conserved.
Any force should describe a Lorentz-type force
if formulated as a sole effect of symplectic perturbations.

5.3
Gravity with Spin

Covariant Poisson Geometry

The general-relativistic spinning particle
is a Hamiltonian system
whose phase space
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{A}_{2}{\>=\>}(T^{*}{\,\oplus\,}\Pi T^{\mathbb{C}}){\mathcal{M}}

describes
real bosonic variables
x^{\mu}
,
p_{m}

and a complex fermionic variable
\psi^{m}
.
While
x^{\mu}
and
p_{m}
describe
position and momentum,
the worldline fermion
\psi^{m}
implements
spin
S_{mn}{\>=\>}{-2i\hskip 1.00006pt{\smash{\bar{\psi}}\kern 0.20004pt}\vphantom{\psi}_{[m}\psi_{n]}}

as a composite object.
